| D170 | Handing a plan to the tribe | Design | Developers | Leaning | Agreed for now, perhaps to be revisited, after the developers suggested switching from blueprint to build: the hammer has two modes. In Build mode, the default, what you lay is a plan as today, and the tribe carries for it at once, so a single wall needs no extra step. In Plan mode, what you lay is a draft the tribe leaves alone. Holding a key at a draft turns its whole building (D155) into plans; holding it at a plan nothing has been put into turns it back. Plan mode gets copying and other tools for drafts. Drafts show only to those building, with the hammer in hand, and to anyone building within the claim. | Asked by the developers on 2026-10-12: recruits must not start a blueprint "while you're still designing it", and "i need your help coming up with a plan here that is a good player experience". Staking out each building was proposed first; the developers: "it makes sense for one big construction, but what if you're just adding one wall inside? maybe its like you switch from blueprint to build? need to think it through". After Dragon Quest Builders 2, Manor Lords, RimWorld and The Sims, which separate designing from the go-ahead, and against Palworld, which builds the moment a plan is laid. Then, of the two modes: "i think but we may have to revisit this"; and, as an idea, Plan mode might become a different kind of building mode, with a camera that is not your character's, perhaps from above. Then: "copying and other features are extremely nice especially since all we're doing is placing blueprints"; and "drafts/blueprints/whatever, would only show when you're in build mode, and should be visible to anyone in build mode within the claim i guess", build mode read as having the hammer in hand. See [tribe.md](tribe.md#plan-and-build-handing-work-to-the-tribe). |
| D297 | Recruits at a build | Design | — | Open | D161 says recruits help by carrying to a build, and perhaps building (Leaning); nothing of it is built, as there are no recruits who take work yet: a freed prisoner follows you and a smith works a forge, nothing more (`play::people`). Recommended: since building draws on storage (D141, D295), small materials are never hauled to a plan, and recruits carry only big ones (D296) from where they lie or are piled to the plans the tribe works on (D170), as RimWorld's colonists haul to blueprints and Sons of the Forest's companion fetches logs; recruits put parts in only once that has been played, as D161 leaves Leaning. First, the tribe's list of work (D162, [tribe.md](tribe.md#who-does-what)). | For the developers: what recruits do at a build, and in what order the tribe's work comes, are design. It waits on D296, and on recruits who take work. |
| D324 | Town life in code | Tech | Agent | Decided | A person's day is a schedule of activities by role in time windows, as Skyrim's packages are, each done at smart objects that advertise what can be done there, where to stand and which clip plays (The Sims; The Witcher 3's pooled spots), so a new activity is data. People walk a settlement's own path graph, from its layout (streets, plot paths, doors), by A*, with ORCA avoidance ported from RVO2 (Apache-2.0); a navmesh comes with collision (D53). They are simulated by distance as creatures are (D145); beyond it, where one is and what they do is a function of the clock and their schedule, and nothing ticks. They are drawn in tiers: full within 25 m; reduced to 80 m (animation at a lower rate, interpolated, fewer joints, the outfit merged); far to 200 m, instanced and posed from vertex animation textures. | Shipped towns run so: Assassin's Creed Unity carries 10,000 people on 40 full AIs and 120 high-detail models, and Kingdom Come: Deliverance II keeps 2,400 NPCs' routines through AI level of detail. No navigation crate supports Bevy 0.20, and the layout already knows every street; a graph is cheap on a server (D52). Our crowd measured bound by the CPU's animation and transforms ([research](../research/bevy-character-capabilities.md)), so the tiers save CPU first. See [demos.md](demos.md#3-a-living-town). |
| D330 | A market town's layout | Tech | Agent | Decided | `terra_build::town` lays a market town from its seed and the ground under it, as Emilien et al. lay villages (2012): a square on the flattest ground (long, a triangle where three roads meet, or a widened street), its long side along the contour; streets traced out of it a step at a time, each turned the way the ground is most level, a side street climbing or falling far enough for a row of plots first; plots cut along every street's sides and the square's edges, each building one of the village rules' three hundred in the seed's order, its door to the way and its front set back by its own wings; a yard behind each house only where a lane along its side reaches it, holding a goat pen of low walls, hens, or a woodshed and a chopping block; the things a day is done at (well, trough, stalls, forge, blocks, benches, places to gather, gates and guard posts) placed by the same rules; and the path graph people walk built from all of it, every edge clear of anything built by 0.45 m. `cargo xtask town` draws the plan. | The town demo (D321) needs a town made by rules from the building system as it stands (D108, D236), and life needs a path graph the layout already knows (D324). Three square shapes by two or three streets by the ground make a few dozen towns a player can tell apart (D182); a yard no one can walk to is no yard. See [demos.md](demos.md#3-a-living-town). |
| D344 | A town's ground worn, its smoke grey, its people carrying their tools and going round visitors | Tech | Agent | Decided | Amends D324 and D325. The ground cover says how trodden a place is (`Growing::trodden`, the cover's second map's alpha); the ground shader lays a settlement's ways as packed earth of their own grey-brown (linear 0.15, 0.132, 0.108), its scan's grain kept but not its hue, stones worked up, mud dark in hollows along the middle and straw at the edges and in yards, over the planet's soil. A hearth's smoke has a grey's albedo (0.3) and a card a wisp's opacity (0.2), and a house cooks on a big fire or a small one by its own luck, so a few columns stand over the roofs. What someone holds is data by activity (`holds` in `townsfolk.ron`: the smith's hammer, the woodcutter's axe, the guard's spear), carried on the way and at it in the hand's socket. Players and the camera are bodies the town's walkers go round by ORCA (`Life::visitors`), and someone out of doors is a fixed body a player stops at. Measured against `town-market-day` (eight KCD1, KCD2 and Witcher 3 frames). | The adversarial review of 2026-10-15 scored the town 4/10: one orange trodden soil, red/green 1.32 against the reference's 1.00, shade red/green 2.3 against 1.13, lit litter 36% against 8% (the planet's warm soil under the cover's duff, its contrast drawn out); white pillars of smoke brighter than the land at dusk (an albedo of 0.62 lit from every side); nobody carrying anything; the camera into a face and a player through people. |
| D346 | A town's furnishing by rules | Tech | Agent | Decided | `terra_build::town` lays, after the yards and by its numbers in `FURNISHING`: a stone trough along the square's edge nearest the well, out of the streets' mouths; two barrels beside the forge, barrels beside some stalls and against the backs of some yards; hand carts in woodpile yards along a side (one beside the market jammed its crowd, so none there yet); and fences round every yard that is not a pen, wattle round hens and split rails round woodpiles, as modules of 2 and 2.5 m with a gate's gap where the lane comes in, its last step then on inside. Each is placed only where it keeps clear of everything built, every thing, place to stand, street, lane and fence; one the path graph cannot reach is taken away. The game draws each thing as its catalogue item and each fence module fitted between its ends along +X, the model contract's module (D303), filed among the obstacles as a capsule, so the player and every body are stopped by it. The fence modules are built in Blender from the kits' own `log_wood` and `wattle` materials (`tools/props/fence.py`); the trough, barrel, cart and the forge, remade in the current style, are Tripo props (D304). | The town review (round 2) found no fences, carts, barrels or trough, and the forge a flat-colour block-out of the old direction (D14). Shipped towns furnish their streets so: Kingdom Come's and The Witcher 3's yards are fenced and their markets stood about with barrels and carts. Fences of the building kits' own materials match the houses they stand behind. |
| D352 | A market stall's goods from CC0 scans, by recipe | Tech | Agent | Decided | For D350's stalls with varied goods: what a stall shows (baskets of apples, onions and pears, crates of roots and rolls, bread on boards, jars and pots, wooden bowls and plates, tubs, axes and tools, bolts and folds of cloth, brass pans and goblets) is made from Poly Haven's CC0 photoscanned models with their own textures, not generated: each scan cut to 50 to 800 triangles and its maps to 1K or 512 (D304), a woven basket that decimation tears apart remeshed as a shell with its scan baked into it, glazes and CC0 fabrics dyed, cloth wound into bolts and folded into pads in Blender, and composed into one prop a good by a recipe in `art/goods.json` (`tools/props/goods.ts`): pieces stood or laid, and fruit and bread heaped into a container by Blender's rigid bodies onto a false floor, only the top layer modelled, as games fill a crate. 28 goods, 530 to 2,400 triangles each, made to the model contract (D303) and in the catalogue's hidden `Goods`. No CC0 scan of meat or cheese was found, so neither is sold yet. | Scans of real fruit, bread, pottery and woodware read as real things beside the textured town (D14) for nothing, where a Tripo model a good would cost 50 credits each; composing them by recipe makes many goods from a few dozen scans, and a heap settled by physics looks piled, not placed. |

## Building

How structures are planned, built, drawn and coded.

| # | Topic | Owner | By | Status | Decision | Why, or what closes it |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| D48 | Construction | Design | Developers | Decided | Building rests on each base's voxel grid, as Enshrouded's does (D181): parts are walls, floors and roofs (D152), placed the Sims' way (D154). On that basis, the improvements in [building.md](building.md#enshrouded-or-subnautica-how-placing-works): bases that matter, building on round worlds, building together, and perhaps structural strength. | Said by the developers on 2026-10-11: "Subnautica 2 is just easier, but as long as the basis is almost like this voxel grid we could have flexibility in time", agreeing with the improvements. Was Leaning: Subnautica 2's shaped rooms as the inspiration (2026-10-10), then under review; rooms went on 2026-10-12 (D152). |
| D67 | Build menu | Design | Developers | Decided | Like Subnautica: you equip a building tool, and it brings up a build menu of what you can build. | Said by the developers on 2026-10-10. Replaces "a menu on B" from earlier the same day. Built in the game: a tabbed menu of tiles with the hammer in hand, and a wheel for one thing's kinds. |
| D108 | One system for everything built | Design | Developers | Decided | Everything built in a world, the player's and the people's alike, is made of the same parts and components: a village's houses are put up from the build catalogue by the same operations, and props and stations such as a well are buildable things too. There are no prefab buildings: a smithy is a building put up from parts with a forge and anvil placed in it. Only stations and props are single models. Not everything seen need be buildable at first; some may be learned or unlocked (D68), how being open. | Said by the developers on 2026-10-11: "everything in the world should be fundamentally built on the same components", for the tech demo as well. See [building.md](building.md#one-system-for-everything-built). |
| D131 | Building on uneven ground | Design | Developers | Decided | A structure set into the ground cuts the ground away only inside its footprint, down to its floor, no deeper than its own height; under the floor stays, for the foundation, and outside a building the ground is not changed (narrowed by D193, 2026-10-12). Building below ground is D143. Trees and rocks where it would stand are not cleared by what is built: the player clears them first, and until then it cannot be placed. Small plants and grass are cleared as before. | Recommended on 2026-10-11 and agreed by the developers as reasonable ("placing a room could let it cut away"); "we should prevent you from placing buildings where there are props like trees/rocks and make you clear those first". Built in the game. The ground cut away gives nothing yet. |
| D139 | Structures are planned, then constructed | Design | Developers | Decided | A structure is laid out as a plan that costs nothing, then actually constructed part by part with materials and work; doors and windows are carved out of walls, slightly differently. | D295 | Storage, and building from it | Tech | Agent | Decided | As step 7 of [building-plan.md](building-plan.md) builds D141 on D84's storage: storage is an item set down from the build menu with the cells it `holds` (`builds.ron`; a chest of pine and iron, 8 by 6, for six Wood), its grid a `Storage` component on it, saved with it (`play::storage`). Whatever is built in a claim, a plan's parts and the items set down, is paid from the builder's pack first and then from the storage standing in that claim, nearest the builder first (`Stores`, through `build::act::Building`); what taking apart gives back still goes to the pack. F opens a chest beside the backpack, a click moving a thing across and Shift and a click every one of its kind (`storage_screen`). Storage holding anything is neither taken apart nor moved. Driven by script with `storage.place`, `storage.put` and `storage.state`, and checked by `cargo xtask cabin`. | Mine, building step 7 on 2026-10-14. | D144 | Building controls | Design | Developers | Decided | The building controls and snapping in [building.md](building.md#controls): a click lays a plan for free and work spends materials; height on Shift and the wheel, kind of construction on Ctrl and the wheel, undo on Y (Enshrouded's; Ctrl and Z would roll), drawing walls and rooms by dragging; right mouse the build menu, Q take apart, G move, F to use and open doors; snapping to joins, then the grid, then surfaces, sticky and gliding. | D154 | How placing walls, floors and roofs works | Design | Developers | Decided | The Sims' way with our materials: the player draws the shape (walls at any length and height, floors, storeys, openings, a roof's style and pitch over what is enclosed); the game makes the details (how each wall's logs or stones are laid, corners, trims, the roof's rafters and ridge). More shapes, and placing details by hand, come with progress (amended by D226: building by hand, roofs too, from the start). Chosen over Enshrouded's way (any shape, every detail by hand: free, but overwhelming) and Subnautica's (fixed modules: easy, but not unique). | Asked by the developers on 2026-10-12: "like enshrouded style, or subnautica style. i do like enshroudeds flexibility to design something truly interesting/unique/beautiful but i will admit its also a little overwhelming". The developers find The Sims "not bad" but asked what was proposed; written out as building a log cabin, which they agreed: "oh yeah i like what you're proposing… it seems good", wondering how hard it is technically with many variants of walls and the rest. See [building.md](building.md#enshrouded-or-subnautica-how-placing-works). |
| D155 | What a building is | Design | Developers | Decided | The game knows its buildings: the walls, floors and roofs that touch one another are one building, and the spaces they enclose are its rooms. Stations belong to their building, and their expansions count anywhere in it (D151). How weather and warmth act indoors is D78's and D153's. | Said by the developers on 2026-10-12: "lets make them count anywhere in the building … buildings will need to know if they're 'complete' (aka walls/roof) anyways because otherwise you have natural elements like rain/cold that should have effect". | D160 | How structures work in code | Tech | Agent | Decided | What stands is kept by `terra_build` as half-metre cubes of a kind of construction on the base's grid (D181), saved and sent as operations; each cube is planned or built to a fill level, which is the unit of progress, cost and collision; a part is how the interface groups cubes (since 2026-10-12). Pieces, what is wall and what floor (D277), buildings, rooms in three dimensions (D261) and collision are worked out from the cubes by pure functions, for the building that changed. Each kind is drawn by its kit (patterns in code, kits as data, D177, D258), each exposed edge by its role (D215), and always follows the current kit, so a kit tuned later redraws what is built (since 2026-10-12; was frozen once a piece was in). A roof is cubes of a roof kind, drawn from their neighbours (D241, D258); it was a height field with The Sims' sections for gables. Built pieces are merged into meshes per fixed section of cubes and material, rebuilt where cubes changed, with a simplified stand-in per section far off. A base is saved and sent as a snapshot and the operations since; ground heights under a plan are sampled into it. Saves of any older format are refused, not converted (D353). See [building-tech.md](building-tech.md). | Mine, for D139, D146, D152, D154 and D155, after [research/procedural-buildings.md](../research/procedural-buildings.md). Rule tables over wave function collapse, which can fail. The height field because walls run in four directions, so a hip roof needs no skeleton solver, and none in Rust fits our licence; checked by a test on L, T, U and courtyard plans rather than a separate spike. Progress per cube, not per piece, so a piece's identity can change with its kit and neighbours without losing what was built ([construction-001.md](proposals/construction-001.md#gaps-and-how-to-close-them)). |
| D175 | Sizes of structural parts | Design | Agent | Leaning | Walls, floors, storeys, doors and windows are D181's. | D176 | A plan view from above, and heights not storeys | Design | — | Open | An idea for later, not to be built yet: Plan mode (D170) lifting the camera into a view over the claim for drafting, while building stays in the character's view; and, since voxels allow rooms of any height, a cutting plane raised half a metre at a time and stopping at floors, in place of storeys, with each wall its own height. See [building.md](building.md#ideas-for-later). | The developers asked on 2026-10-12 whether planning from the character's camera could be improved ("its not always fun to do it from the character camera"), surveyed in [research/planning-mode-cameras.md](../research/planning-mode-cameras.md); then pointed out that with voxels and variable heights "theres not really stories"; then: "for now lets keep it as an idea/improvement that we may make, but lets not build it yet". |
| D177 | How kit pieces are made and drawn | Tech | Agent | Decided | One layout per wall, by its kit's rule, serves the blueprint, the part going up and the finished building: the same pieces drawn as a ghost, as some in and some ghost, and merged. Drawn from real materials by code: a kind's cubes' surface, roofs' coverings among them (D197, D258), logs (D200), and timber decks, stairs and boarded shapes (D206, D220, D231) until they take modules. A kit is a file in `assets/data/kits/`. A kit sheet on the yard shows every situation in every state. See [building-kits.md](building-kits.md#how-pieces-are-made-d177-d197-d200-d251). Amended by D290: rule-shaped geometry wearing a hand-made model's bake is allowed, and a kit is a material file over its pattern and pack. | Mine, for D139 and D146; revised 2026-10-12 when the developers saw pieces drawn by code and said they broke the art direction ("you're just drawing random geometry which is IN VIOLATION OF OUR ART DIRECTION"). The first version made logs in code, from numbers and a seed, because a log's length is set by its wall; stretching a model along its length does that without inventing its look, since a flat-coloured log has no texture to smear. |
| D178 | How a log wall looks | Design | Agent | Decided | Round logs, made to look hewn and grown rather than turned: uneven sections, taper, bend, knots, axe-cut ends, colour per face from the wood roles, chinking. 0.3 m thick, 12 courses to a storey, saddle-notched at corners, their ends at the corner cube's outer face (they ran 0.25 m past until 2026-10-12, which got in the way of what was built beside them); the two axes half a course apart; split logs at the foot and top of one axis; posts where two walls meet in line; openings framed, with the logs beside them cut. What a wall costs is set apart from its pieces: counted by length, and one carried log may set two short pieces. See [building-kits.md](building-kits.md#logs-d178). | Left to me by the developers on 2026-10-12: "for visuals lets just keep on theme, you can decide, but having 'visual interest' is good and organic when possible", and "we dont need 1:1 the raw materials to visually look the same in the blueprint… placing one log could place 2". Was Open, proposed the same day. |
| D179 | Posts, and mixing kinds of construction | Design | Developers | Decided | As [building-kits.md](building-kits.md#posts-anywhere-d179) says: a post is a part on any grid point, which walls meet as at an in-line join, at a corner giving a corner-post look, in the open holding up a porch; every part has its own kind, and where kinds meet the heavier runs through (stone, logs, planks, wattle); a wall over a lower one of another kind makes a footing. | Asked by the developers on 2026-10-12: "add a pillar to break up walls… arbitrarily?" and "how do you have one wall be log and one be stone". Agreed the same day: "seems fine", posts "at the snap points… on the grid". |
| D181 | Structures strictly on the voxel grid | Design | Developers | Decided | Everything built is half-metre cubes of a kind of construction on its base's grid, as D48 says: walls are rows of cubes a cube thick, posts columns of them, floors layers, openings cubes left empty, of any size and shape, with doors (1 by 2 m) and windows (sill 1 m, head 2 m) as default sizes, Enshrouded's way. Roofs are cubes too, cut to slopes and built by hand, since D226 and D241; they were first the one exception, a surface over the cubes. Kits draw the cubes as their pieces (D146, D177), so logs are still seen and built one by one. Height is counted in cubes; a storey of 3 m is a wall's default height. See [building-tech.md](building-tech.md#the-model). | The developers on 2026-10-12: "i think it sould be strictly aligned to the voxel grid, no?", then "Yes" to writing it as the plan, and "windows could be flexible in size tho, this is more just a default like enshrouded". What it costs: walls a cube thick, so rooms are a metre smaller than drawn; floors a cube thick, so 2.5 m clear in a 3 m storey; doors 2.0 m high, not 2.1. | D192 | Rules for construction pieces | Design | Developers | Decided | [construction-001.md](proposals/construction-001.md): Enshrouded's way on our cubes, keeping plans and ghosts. The cube is the truth; nothing leaves its cubes but eaves, footings and trims, which may stand out up to an eighth of a cube (cornices, copings, sills, plinths, quoins), drawn only, never colliding and never stopping a neighbour being placed (2026-10-13); any arrangement is drawn; a piece is chosen by its neighbourhood, long members on runs and blocks on the dual grid; plans are the same cubes, drawn as ghosts and walked through, and only what is built collides; doors and windows are props fitted into holes that fit them; the ground outside a building is not changed; nothing floats. Block kinds go to the dual grid only if its spike shows conformed Tripo modules look as good as the stone sections. | The developers asked for guard rails "similar to how enshrouded does", said "our core needs to be voxels" and that rendering each voxel by its neighbours "seems like a really good system if we can adopt it and keep our build ghosts", asked for every case to be thought through, then answered its questions or left them to me: "make the best default call that we can solve from a technical angle and is a good player experience" (2026-10-12). On trims standing out past their cubes, as Enshrouded's cornices do: "yeah i want it to not be flat, so thats ok to draw stuff" (2026-10-13). |
| D193 | What holds, what falls, and plans' limits | Design | Developers | Decided | Nothing is taken away while anything is attached to it or rests on it: an item on a floor, a roof on its walls, a door in its hole; a player is refused and told what holds it, so nothing has to drop. What loses its support without being asked, to an attack or the ground giving way, comes down; nothing floats. What is destroyed or comes down bursts into fragments of its own material that fall onto the ground, cannot be touched, and fade once they have settled (2026-10-15, amending debris that gave back part of its materials); giving back materials from what comes down is put off while building takes no real resources. A cube is whole, a stair, a slope or a half, more shapes as kinds need them. Ground is cut only inside a building's footprint, down to its floor (D131 narrowed). A claim has a budget of planned cubes, and plans are seen only by its tribe, with the hammer in hand, nearby. Taking apart gives back exactly what went in, to the claim's storage when the taker did not pay for it, and who may take apart is by rank; a cube taken apart goes with its plan, nothing left behind as a blueprint, and undo puts it back (2026-10-15). Claims do not overlap, keep a margin, and everything built, eaves and footings too, stays inside its claim. | The developers on 2026-10-12, answering [construction-001.md](proposals/construction-001.md#open-for-the-developers)'s questions 8 to 14: "broadly we should refuse to remove things with other things attach. otherwise we have to add another system to 'drop' those things"; "i agree collapse into debris"; "cube shapes are fine for stair slope and half"; "i think the rest makes sense". On taking apart, asked whether a building taken apart should stay as blueprints: "it should deconstruct and return materials" (2026-10-15). On destruction, seeing debris piles (2026-10-15): "for destruction it needs to kind of.. 'explode' into voxels if that makes sense.. like a bunch of cubes that fall onto the ground and you cant interact with that might fade when done? like when it falls down or gets destroyed or w/e", "dont scope creep", building with infinite pretend resources for now; and "all the destroy thing needs to be performant AND look sick". |
| D194 | Construction defaults | Design | Agent | Decided | Footings fill down to the ground and stop there, costed by the cube, the floor at the highest ground under the building. What goes in is a cube's fill: a log fills its course in every cube it spans, a block kind goes in a cube at a time, holding the key keeps going. Each kit's corners as its material really is: crisp cut stone and brick, round plaster and earth. Walls of any thickness. Stone, brick, plaster and earth are blocks; logs, timber framing, planks and wattle long members. The game lays footings into the plan as their own part, built first, stone by default, posts, stilts or none by choice. Nothing floats in the MVP (refines D142). | Left to me by the developers on 2026-10-12: "make the best default call that we can solve from a technical angle and is a good player experience", answering [construction-001.md](proposals/construction-001.md#open-for-the-developers)'s questions 1 to 7 with its recommendations. |
| D195 | How the construction system is built | Tech | Agent | Decided | As [building-plan.md](building-plan.md) says: `terra_build` split into modules a concern (cubes in sections of 16, operations with checks and inverses, ground, support, rooms, cost, kits, layouts for runs, blocks, trims, roofs and ground, walking, saves); layouts are pure functions giving piece instances in grid units, so the server needs no art; in the game, a plugin a concern, sections as entities, layouts on the worker pool, built pieces merged per section and material, ghosts instanced, far meshes from the pieces' lowest detail; snapshots plus operations; property tests over random operations, live remote commands and a `village` bench. In order: the block-module spike, the model, layouts, drawing, plans and building, then shapes, loss and repair, materials, networking. | Mine, asked for by the developers on 2026-10-12: "a technology plan to implement the best construction system we can come up with that aligns w/ the notes in our agents.md and other docs". |
| D197 | How block kinds are drawn | Design | Developers | Decided | Stone, brick, plaster and earth are drawn as their cubes' own surface, rounded along every outside edge and displaced by a real material from the pipeline (`terra_build::surface`), as Enshrouded seems to, not as modelled pieces, so any shape, carved anywhere, is one wall with no seams. Long members (logs, beams, planks) stay modelled pieces; doors, windows, trims and details stay props. An exception to D177 for block kinds. Dressed by its roles in pieces of its own materials (D215); walls, floors and roofs all drawn this way (D251). Amended by D290: a block kind is a material of the block pattern, its file a few lines over the pattern and its pack. | The spike (2026-10-12, [research/block-modules.md](../research/block-modules.md)): Tripo's stone cut into pieces chosen by their neighbours read as a grid of metre blocks and broke where a carving interrupted it; the displaced surface read as one natural wall. The developers: "ok that looks pretty good tbqh, ok do that". |
| D199 | The construction model's rules in code | Tech | Agent | Decided | As `terra_build` holds them (step 1 of [building-plan.md](building-plan.md)): a cube's fill counted in sixteenths, so a kind fills in 1, 2, 4, 8 or 16 steps; parts numbered by whoever lays them, through a palette of up to 255 a section, those that touch and agree merged as cubes are laid by hand (D260); *joined* is face to face through cubes with anything in them, and the ground reaches a cube whose foot is at or below its column's sampled height, the base's own level where nothing was sampled; a roof stands on a built cube under every eave, a prop in built cubes across its top, down both sides and under its foot unless on the ground; nothing goes in over a cube partly in, and a cube is not left partly in under one already in, so one under a cube already in, as a breach rebuilt under the wall over it, goes in whole in one act, all its steps taken, and its undo carves it out step by step (2026-10-15, the fourth goals review's finding 3); a carving is marked, taken out step by step, then unplanned, and a change of kind marked, the old taken out, swapped, the new put in; each part keeps the terms it was laid on, so refunds are exact; costs are shares of an item in powers of two, no finer than 1/65536 and no more than 2^24 items, so they add up without overflowing, and a snapshot holding any other amount is refused, as it arrives over the network; undo is the inverse operations, refused like any other, so a cube put in under something now resting on it cannot be undone (D193). The shortcuts stay instant and unchecked, laying built cubes, until plans are laid by the tools (step 4). The cube's layout is D231's since: five bits of shape, two of the pending change, which now also marks a cube swapped to its new kind (D232). | Mine, building the model on 2026-10-12. The choices later steps build on, written down so they are not left in code; property tests over random operations hold them (`ops/tests.rs`). |
| D200 | How the timber kind is drawn | Design | Developers | Decided | Timber is drawn as generated long members (`terra_build::timber`): a profile swept along each run the layout gives, shaped by the kit's numbers and each piece's seed, displaced and textured by real materials from the pipeline, with sawn ends and chinking. Round logs in their bark (A) are the log kit, each log varying by its seed in thickness, taper, bend, the turn of its section and its colour; hewn timber (B) is a second profile of the same code, for beams, planks and squared timber. A new timber kind is a kit file (D136). Not the Tripo log models, which are gone, and not the block kinds' surface (D197). Another exception to D177, as D197 is. See [research/timber-walls.md](../research/timber-walls.md) and [building-kits.md](building-kits.md#how-pieces-are-made-d177-d197-d200-d251). To be compared with hand-made log pieces on D251's system. Amended by D290: timber kinds are materials of the coursed pattern, their numbers in the pattern and their pack. | The developers, shown the spike (2026-10-13) with the recommendation "A, with more variation per log (thickness, taper, bend, colour by seed); B's code kept as a second profile for beams, planks, squared timber": "Ok lets complete the task. Earlier they asked for timber that "doesn't have to be a literal log if we can't make that work but it has to be thematic and look beautiful", procedural and extensible as data. |
| D201 | Plans and building in the game | Tech | Agent | Decided | As step 4 of [building-plan.md](building-plan.md) builds it: every change the hammer, a script or the remote makes to the player's base is a `terra_build` operation through one checked path (`build::act`), with the claim's square of columns and its budget of planned cubes (`builds.ron`), the cubes bodies stand in and items stand on, and what the builder carries; openings are planned cubes made open (`Op::Open`, `Op::Close`), so a doorway is in the plan and a door or window, a prop, is fitted into it once the wall round it is built; what goes in next is the lowest of a part not yet in, a course across the part for long members and the nearest cube that can go in for blocks, then roofs a stage at a time; fractions of an item are cut from whole ones and the rest kept as the claim's offcuts, given back as they make whole items; only walls built whole close a room in; the ground is sampled again after a room cuts it (`Op::Ground`), refused where it would undermine what is built; a base is saved as a snapshot and the operations since; a save of another version is refused (D353). The room shortcut lays its own parts only where nothing is, and no longer opens walls between rooms. | Mine, building step 4 on 2026-10-13: the choices the drawing and networking steps build on, so they are not left in code. Exact refunds need fractions kept somewhere; the claim's is the least a player sees. |
| D206 | Footings, floors and steps in code | Tech | Agent | Decided | As `terra_build` lays them (D194): footings are cubes of the kind of the floor they carry where that is a block kind, as a stone floor stands on stone, else of the plan's footing kind, the first block kit's (stone); their own part, laid first by a plan: under every ground-storey wall from the floor's layer, and under a floor's edges from the layer below, each column down to the cube the ground passes through, measured at the lowest of its corners less a few centimetres, at most eight cubes; a room's floor is laid inside its footing; on a layer the part aimed at goes in first, then the part laid first, then the nearest, so a footing is whole before its floor. A storey below the ground storey closes a room in only where it has a floor, so the space a footing rings is none. The shortcuts lay footings as built cubes from the planet's ground. A room's cut in the ground stops at its walls' outer faces. A block kind draws all its cubes, walls, floors and footings, as one surface, its tops in the kit's `Floor` material (flagstones for stone); a timber kind's floors and flat roofs are decks from the timber code: two sawn boards to a cube's width, joints staggered over joists and run on under walls to the logs' faces, a rim on every open side, joists where the underside is in the air and bearers over its posts (D306), each board drawn from one plank of its texture (`strips`). Door steps are cubes since D261: stairs and halves of the footing kind from each doorway down to the ground, the ground not changed. | Mine, building footings, floors and steps on 2026-10-13: the grey slab and steps under every build stood in for them, and footings were not in plans. Footings as cubes make support, cost and order the rules' own; steps as cubes wait for stairs (D193's shapes). |
| D207 | Openings cut into what is built, and doors that swing | Tech | Agent | Decided | An opening asked for where a wall is built marks its cubes to be carved (`Op::Carve`); building then takes them out a course at a time from the top, a cube at a time for blocks, giving back what went in, and makes them open; a cube being carved may come out from under what is over it, which stays, held by the wall either side as a lintel spans it, so long as everything stays joined, and nothing else rests on it (a roof's eaves, a prop, an item); a carving left half done under something cannot be cancelled. Layouts draw a cube as an opening from its first cut, framed. A door's model was cut into a `Frame` that stays and a `Leaf` that swings on the low edge of its own box; no door model is used since D223. | Mine, on 2026-10-13: construction-001's fourth experience carves a window out of a built wall, the logs cut and framed and given back, which D199's last in, first out refused; and the door swung with its frame. |
| D208 | Pieces placed in grid units | Tech | Agent | Decided | A layout's pieces are placed in the base's grid in ticks of a 256th of a cube, about 2 mm (`layout::Place`, `layout::TICKS`), not metres: points, boxes and lengths along a piece are whole numbers of ticks, and only drawing turns them into metres. A piece's version is still a seed hashed from its place. | Mine, for [building-plan.md](building-plan.md)'s rule that a layout gives piece instances in grid units, so what machines share holds no floating point a machine could round otherwise; a power of two of a cube, so the grid's lines and the courses' heights are exact, and fine enough that no piece is seen to move. |
| D210 | Trims where openings and kinds meet, and the kit checker | Tech | Agent | Decided | Round every opening in a block kind's wall, where its kit has a `Dressed` stone, a dressed lintel where the wall goes on above, a sill under a window and jamb stones up its sides, long and short by turns, each a little proud of the rubble (`layout::blocks`); without one, a timber lintel. Where a thinner wall of another kind stands on blocks, as logs on a stone plinth or footing, a dressed cap over the blocks' top; where a timber wall ends against another kind's, an end board up the joint over the logs' sawn ends (`layout::trims`). Log walls keep their frames of boards. `cargo xtask kits`, run by `cargo xtask check`, checks every kit file (`terra_build::checker`): the materials its pattern needs and every map of each, life-size numbers, costs in items there are, and the style guide's budgets measured on a cabin laid in it: 700 triangles a metre of log and 500 a square metre of roof. Since D215, the stone round openings and the caps are a kit's dress by role, and the checker checks the dress too. | Mine, for step 2 of [building-plan.md](building-plan.md) on 2026-10-13: the trims and the checker the plan lists; dressed stone from Poly Haven's CC0 `rock_boulder_dry`, read from the kit like every material, and the budgets set over what the three kits draw today. |
| D211 | The look of building: on par with Enshrouded | Design | Developers | Decided | Building takes an approach to voxels like Enshrouded's, and is to be on par with it in how buildings blend together, not a copy of its look: our art style (D14), our kits and our materials. Cubes stay the truth and stay optimised cubes, merged per section; cubes that touch blend into one structure, with no seams or stacked boxes; and each cube's look changes with what it touches and where it sits (corner, edge, top, base, rough or dressed, against another material, against the ground), as Enshrouded's blocks do. This is the bar every kind, the stone surface (D197), generated timber (D200), roofs (D209) and trims (D210), is judged against, side by side with Enshrouded, and changed where it falls short. Finding the technology that meets it is the agent's: research first ([research/voxel-building-look.md](../research/voxel-building-look.md)), then spikes compared against Enshrouded, then the chosen way built and measured. | The developers on 2026-10-13: "so enshrouded has cubes right, and then when two cubes touch, it somehow blends it. thats really what we're after"; "they change visually depending on what they're touching or where they're located"; "make sure this is clear that that is our agenda in the plan and you need to find the technology solution to achieve it"; "we need the system to be ON PAR WITH ENSHROUDED"; "this doesnt mean we're copying enshrouded, but we want to take an approach to voxels similar to how they've done it for the way buildings blend together with textures/whatever". |
| D213 | How bases are drawn | Tech | Agent | Decided | As step 3 of [building-plan.md](building-plan.md#drawing) builds it: a base is an entity and each section of its cubes, 16 a side, a child entity; every piece belongs to the section of the cube its middle is in, worked out in ticks, and a section holds exactly what the base laid whole has there, cut from it (D238). A change sets waiting the sections it marks and those its pieces reach (`sections::reach`, D281); each is laid and merged on Bevy's async compute pool, nearest the camera first, its parts drawn again after an edit side by side on a few spare threads, a part keyed by what is drawn within a cube of it and the rain over its own columns (a cube shapes only the faces of the cubes beside it), and a section whose cubes an edit filled or emptied drawn in parts an eighth as big (`sections::FINE_CLUSTER`), so a step built puts less on the GPU again; the base it is laid from copied for the workers in a few thousand steps, its sections', columns' and parts' cubes shared until changed; a change's meshes are put on the GPU a budget of corners a frame and the change swapped in once all of it is there, as a streaming world uploads a chunk before it shows it; and a section left as it is if it lays the same (`Laid::key`). Built pieces merge into a mesh per section, kind and material, long members and block surfaces sharing their corners; a block surface is worked out a cube past its section's edge, so sections meet with no seam. A plan's pieces are ghosts, a unit box or eight-sided prism each stretched over the piece, merged on the worker into a mesh for each of two ghost materials, drawn as a blueprint (each face's edges as lines, `shaders/ghost.wesl`), to 90 m; the next part is drawn as it will be, lit. Every piece as made to 25 m; to 60 m a textured mid detail, members of eight sides and stone in four steps; past 60 m one mesh a section, coarser members and stone, roofs a plain covering, its textures and their occlusion baked into its corners' colours; past 180 m coarser again, to 600 m; `VisibilityRange` fades each into the next. Finer detail is made only near the camera (full within 32 m, textured within 70 m) and dropped as it leaves. A room closed and covered holds a light probe of the sky dimmed to 60% (D155; the camera is exposed for daylight, so less reads as black). The loading screen waits for the buildings in the world to be drawn. Measured by `cargo xtask bench village` against `village-bare`. | Mine, building step 3 on 2026-10-13: a cabin's redraw took 20 ms on the main thread and the whole kit sheet could not be laid at once; whole-base relayout and an entity per piece had to go. Laying by building rather than only the marked sections, with sections that lay the same left alone, is exact for what reaches across a building (a run of logs' joins, a roof's kind from its walls, a deck's boards) and still merges again only what changed. Bevy batches entities sharing a mesh and material, so a ghost per piece costs a draw per material, not per piece. Texture arrays were not needed: a section near is ten or so draws, far off one. |
| D215 | Dressing a kit's edges by their role | Design | Developers | Decided | Each exposed face, edge and corner of a kit's cubes has a role worked out from the cubes and the ground's samples when a section is laid, never stored (`terra_build::layout::roles`): an arris up an outside corner, an inside corner, a jamb up an opening's side, a crest along a top, a nose along a step, a sill and a head round an opening, a soffit under an overhang, a foot where it stands on the ground or another kind or the ground passes it, a border along a floor it stands on, a meet where a thinner kind stands on it; each edge belongs to one cube, so a piece laid for it is in, planned or carved with that cube. A kit's file dresses each role (`dress`): a profile from its pieces swept along the edge a cube at a time, mitred out and back at corners, jointed and capped (`Piece::Trim`); corner stones a course at a time, long and short by turns (`Piece::Corner`); or a long member of its timber (a plate, a sill, a post, a step's nose and stringers). Profiles and corner stones are made in Blender from a source under `art/kits/` (`tools/kits`) into a RON file the kit names; a foot steps with the ground a course at a time and runs on down to it; masks shade what a kit draws by the ground's dirt, worn edges and weathering. Pieces stand out up to an eighth of a cube (D192). Extends D197 and D200 under D211; the stored cube, sections, operations and saves do not change. Whole modules per face (the spike's fourth candidate) stay possible as a refined kit's option within the same roles. Every piece is of the raw materials its kind is built from, so stone is dressed in stone and logs in timber; a piece of another material puts that material in the kind's cost (earth's oak lintels make wood part of earth's), and `cargo xtask kits` checks it. Amends D197: a block kind is its cubes' surface dressed by its roles. Extended by D258 to roofs and frames: modules along lines and stones at points, besides profiles. Amended by D290: the roles' pieces are the pattern's, shared by its materials and drawn in each one's bake. | Mine, building step 2a of [building-plan.md](building-plan.md) from the role pieces spike ([research/role-pieces.md](../research/role-pieces.md)): of the four candidates, profiles and corner pieces over the surface reached the bar for stone, carved, joined and built part by part with no special cases, sat on the cube store and layouts as they are, and cost about one draw per kind per section and a few hundred triangles a cube near; a new look is a kit file and its source. The developers ratified it on 2026-10-13, asked whether it should stand: "whatever thematically makes sense. they need to ideally generally come from the raw materials we'll use to build something". |
| D220 | Cubes cut to shapes, posts, and timber masses | Tech | Agent | Decided | As step 5 of [building-plan.md](building-plan.md) builds D193's shapes: a cube is whole, a half (its lower half), a stair (two steps, each half its height and depth, rising toward a side), a slope (a ramp up to a side) or a post (code 10 of its shape bits, five since D231): a post is a column's cube drawn as an upright as narrow as its kit dresses `Role::Post` (a member of its timber, or `Pillar(width)` of its stone), and holds as a whole cube; a walker meets it as a box 36 cm square (`walk::POST_M`), not its cube (2026-10-13). A walker stands on a shape's top at the point under them (`Shape::top`), what is under the middle of their feet out to 60% of their radius, so a 25 cm step is climbed as the body reaches it, as a capsule climbs; a wall is met where the walker crosses into the next column. Support, cost, order and taking apart are a whole cube's (D199): joined face to face, a shaped cube costing what a whole one of its kind does, its offcut lost in the cutting. A block kind draws halves and slopes as its surface cut down to their tops (`surface`'s cut), stairs as a stone a tread and posts as a drum a cube (`surface::slab`), and dresses their lines with bands of its profiles (`Piece::Band`): a nose on each tread, a coping round a half's open top and raking up a slope's open sides, a plinth round a pillar's base and a coping round its cap. A timber kind lays a column topped by a stair or a slope, whatever is set on it, as a step of its flights (`layout::stairs`), open stairs as a carpenter builds them: a thick tread on each step, or a ramp of boards up the fall with a cleat, housed between board stringers up both sides from the floor to the top step's back, posts under the stringers where their span needs one (D306), nothing under the treads (the cubes under them still walked as cubes); halves as decks half a cube high; posts as one upright on a pad of boards, under a bolster where something rests on them, under a deck's bearer where a deck does (D306). A solid mass of a timber kind (whole cubes of a wall in a block two by two on a layer) is a framed deck: its top boards on joists with a rim of beams stacked a course deep, its inside nothing, its sides walls; the top layer of a wall tile over a post is what the post carries, not a wall. Slope cubes are not roofs: D209's roofs are laid from roof sections over walls, and a slope is a ramp of its own kind's material, under or beside a roof as any cube is. The hammer lays them as parts (`build::plans`): a flight of stairs or a ramp up to the floor of the storey above, a post to it, each on a footing on the ground, a floor drawn alone, and one block of a shape against the face aimed at, joining the plan of its kind beside it; the wheel turns which way a flight or a cut block rises. Roofs take a kit's masks: the covering greened by moss and lichen toward its eaves (`moss`, `moss_tint`), greyed toward its ridge by `weather`, its eaves and verges worn lighter. | Mine, building step 5 on 2026-10-13: Keen's platform and slab drew as piles of log ends; shapes existed in the cube store and the operations but walked as whole cubes and drew as nothing of their own. A stone a tread and a drum a cube are how stone stairs and pillars are built; cutting the surface keeps halves and slopes one surface with what they meet. |
| D225 | Doors and shutters as props; windows open | Design | Developers | Decided | Doors and windows' shutters are props fitted into a hole that fits them (D192, D201), made again in the style (D14) after the first ones were taken out as not good enough: three doors (plank, ledged and braced, studded), one leaf in a doorway 1 m wide and a pair in one 1.5 or 2 m wide, and two shutters (board, louvred), a pair over a window wider than half a metre; each a kind in `builds.ron` with what a leaf costs, a pair two. A door hangs in its hole's reveal inside the frame the kit lays round it, which stays, and swings a quarter turn into the room (D207); shutters hang on the wall's outside and fold back against it; a leaf stops a walker where it stands, shut, open or swinging. The hammer fits them once the walls round the hole are built, shows them as a ghost first, and takes them out for what went in; the interact key opens and shuts them; the village has them again. Windows have no glass: a window's cubes are open, as a doorway's are, and collide as open cubes do; a window prop is shutters and the kit's frame, never glass. | The developers on 2026-10-13: "we have some of these ugly ass door and window props we can probably just remove for now", "until better props are made"; "also remove the glass windows which i think still exist (unless im wrong). just leave windows as a hole in the wall". Before that: "windows could also be props if we need them to be more than voxels … so they can actually look nice and be interactive", "same w/ doors - so you'd just need a hole that fits them. same as enshrouded". On bringing them back, 2026-10-14: "Like get it done", approval delegated, nothing to ship that reads cheap. |
| D226 | Building by hand, roofs too | Design | Developers | Decided | Players build by hand as in Enshrouded: brushes of 1, 2 and 4 m, carving any box, taking out the cube aimed at, and roofs built by hand, cube by cube, so a roof can take any shape and storeys can be added over it; ridges, eaves, verges, hips and valleys form from the roof cubes' neighbours as walls' trims do (D215). The game's quick ways stay as conveniences: laying a room, a wall, and "add a roof with this material" over what is enclosed, which lays roof cubes like any other. All unlocked from the start for now, for testing. Amends D154, which chose the Sims' way, and changes how D209 lays roofs. Amended by D343: nothing but a roof is built on a roof; a storey goes up with the roof taken off and put on again. | The developers on 2026-10-13: on brushes, "it doesnt matter, but for the sake of testing we'll just have it unlocked"; on roofs, "we want you to be able to customize the roof, or add more stories, which means you gotta be able to manually build the roof. the auto roof thing was just from the sims its not our goal (though we could have it as a behavior.. like you could 'auto add a roof with [x material]')". |

| D229 | The village laid out from its seed | Design | Agent | Decided | The MVP's settlement (D108) is a street village made by rules in `terra_build::village`, not placed by hand: a street east and west with a green and the well on it, houses along both sides a few metres apart with their doors to the street, a woodshed, a lookout deck on posts with its stair toward the street, and gardens walled in stone behind two houses, each length of wall its own build so it steps with the ground. A building is a plan (rectangle, L, T, U, two storeys with a stair up the outside of a gable end to a landing on a post, shed, deck, low wall) in a scheme of the kits' kinds (logs; stone; hewn timber; logs on a stone plinth a metre high; a stone ground floor under hewn timber; log walls with stone gable ends; cob; wattle and daub; a stone ground floor under wattle and daub, its floor and stair of hewn timber) under a roof (gable, hip, lean-to; an L, a T or a U under gables or hips, never a lean-to, its wings' ridges dying into the main roof), at a width; its windows, pitch, a lean of a few degrees and a step along the street come from the village's seed. That is about three hundred buildings told apart by plan, scheme, roof and width (`village::variants`); the village has twelve houses, a shed, a deck and eight lengths of wall, every plan and scheme at least once. The village is set down, 75 to 115 m from the start and well away from the lair, where its street and every building's door, back and corners stand on the most level dry ground, its street turned to suit. Each is put up by the shortcuts in its own cubes, its door's middle at its origin, stood where it goes and lifted onto its footings; it gives the world its footprint, so trees, bushes and the forest floor keep out of it and a margin round it, grown before or after it, and the street is cleared and trodden bare. It logs where to stand for its views, so `cargo xtask demo` takes the same pictures of it every time. A roof the shortcuts put up is of the kind at the top of the walls under it. Whatever stands on the ground storey's foot, a post, a flight, a low wall, stands on footings like a wall. It is test content: procedural villages are designed later. | The developers on 2026-10-13: "complete the construction tech implementation fully for our demo, with the two core materials (log-based and stone-based), and the varying builds of it", on par with Enshrouded (D211); trees grew up through the village's four flat-roofed log houses. | D231 | More shapes, and a cube's shape in five bits | Tech | Agent | Decided | Amends D199's cube layout and adds to D220's shapes, as [research/enshrouded-parity.md](../research/enshrouded-parity.md) gap 4 proposes: a cube's shape is five bits (12 to 16; fill 17 to 20, built 21, the pending change 22 and 23, the part 24 to 31), taking the bit the pending change did not use, so 32 codes: whole, half, a stair and a slope each way, post, and now the upper half (11), a stair (12 to 15) and a slope (16 to 19) upside down, and outer (20 to 23) and inner (24 to 27) corner slopes, rising toward a side and the next side clockwise, the lower or the higher of the two ramps; an opening is 31. Snapshots are version 2; any other is refused (D353). Each shape has a top and an underside over each point (`Shape::top`, `Shape::bottom`): a walker stands on its top and passes under its underside. Support, cost, order and taking apart are a whole cube's, as D220's. A block kind cuts its surface down to a planed top and up to a planed underside (`surface`'s cut is a range, each square's diagonal chosen to follow a crease), draws a stair either way up as a stone a step, and dresses each open side's line, level or raking, with a coping above or a fascia below; a timber kind boards them (`layout::shapes`): a deck on a flat top, boards up each ramp cut at the crease, a boarded underside, flat, raking or stepped, and its open sides clad course by course as long as the shape is wide. The shapes are general cubes a roof kind can later be laid on (D226). | Mine, building gap 4 of the parity note on 2026-10-13: the bit was spare, so the shape grew without a palette per section; a range cut gives every planed shape from one mesher, and boxes of boards are how timber builders close a cut shape. |
| D232 | The material in hand, changing it in place, and how kinds meet | Tech | Agent | Decided | The hammer holds a material (`Builder::material`), every plan it lays laid in it: Ctrl and the wheel step through every kit in `assets/data/kits` while a part is in hand, as Enshrouded's do, Ctrl being the roll's key, which waits then, as Shift and the wheel share the sprint's (keys are never shared, D80); the middle button on a cube picks its material; the legend under the aiming mark shows it. *Change material* marks a brush's box of a cube's other kinds to change (`Op::Replace`); building takes the old out a step at a time from under what stays, giving it back, swaps it (`Pending::Swapped`, so the old is kept for undo), builds the new in, and clears the mark (`Base::next_change`), each step refused like any other; a cube being changed, like one carved, may be part in under what stays over it. `Op::Changed` is clearing that mark's inverse. Where kinds meet is each kit's `meets` table: its weight (stone 3, timber 2), the heavier running through a joint and dressed there as though the lighter were not, the lighter ending against it with its `end` trim (`Board`), the heavier carrying its `carries` trim (`Cap`) where a lighter stands on it, and per kind by id what differs; a kit without one meets as its pattern did (D179, D210). A foot over another kind does not step down over it. Amended by D298: between two kinds of one pattern the change is a repaint at once. | Mine, building gaps 1 and 5 of the parity note on 2026-10-13: Ctrl and the wheel is building.md's recommendation and Enshrouded's; the roll and the material both on Ctrl, rather than another key, keeps one key one action and costs only the roll while a part is out. A swapped state rather than inferring it keeps every step of a change exact to undo, as the property tests ask. |
| D233 | Brushes, carving a box and taking out the cube aimed at | Design | Agent | Decided | As D226 asks, in the hammer, on the operations there are: a brush half a metre, 1, 2 or 4 m across (Alt and the wheel, shown in the legend), its box on its own grid holding the cube aimed at, as Enshrouded's larger blocks snap; *Block* and *Corner slope* lay a box of whole cubes with the shape on its top layer (its bottom one for a shape upside down) as one part; *Carve* marks a box's built cubes to be carved out (`Op::Carve`), built then as any carving; take-apart takes a step out of the highest layer of what is in the box aimed at (`Op::Empty`) where nothing rests on it, else what came last of the part, as before. Refusals are the operations' own. The remote has `build.material`, `build.replace`, `build.carve`, `build.take_out` and `build.cube`; `cargo xtask cabin` picks stone, builds a wall, changes a cube in it to logs and lays a corner slope on it. | Mine, on 2026-10-13, with D226: brushes and carving on LayPlan, Carve and Empty need no new operation; snapping a brush to its own grid is Enshrouded's and keeps boxes from overlapping. |
| D236 | Earth, and wattle and daub as a framed kind | Tech | Agent | Decided | D227's kits, as data (D136): **earth** (`earth.ron`) is a block kind drawn as its surface (D197) in ambientCG's CC0 `Ground104` (`packed_earth`, fetched by `tools/textures`' `ambientcg:`), gritty earth with small stones in ridged layers, displaced 7 cm by its own height and its grit in parallax, its yellow taken to a grey-brown by the kit's `tint`, packed in its shape (the kit's `packed`, `surface::Packed`): lumps of a low-frequency noise and each lift rounded out between its seams, which follow the strata's, so it bulges, its lifts read as ridges and its rounded 12 cm edges wander; its lifts a little lighter and darker by turns with a darker seam between (the masks' `strata` and `lift`), weathered by role from its cubes: darker over its own foot (`splash`), streaked under sills and open tops but not under eaves (`streaks`), paler arrises (`wear`) and paler from a storey up, dry under the eaves (`dry`); dressed by role (D215) with a rubble plinth at its foot, a stone coping with a drip on a top open to the sky, and oak lintels, sills, sole plates, fascias and treads (so it costs a little Wood, D215), an opening's head and sill each one run across its full width over its highest row and under its lowest, however ragged the hole (`dressing`), set into the wall, its face bedded behind it (`dress::Bed`) so the lintel sits all but flush and the earth is built up unevenly over its edges (2026-10-14) (a profile's `material`, its texture then running along it as grain), its corners and reveals left round. **Wattle and daub** (`wattle.ron`) is a block kind with a `framing`: its cubes the infill, pale daub (`patterned_clay_plaster`) with a little relief; a frame of squared oak laid over them by rule from the cubes (`layout::frames`, `Piece::Beam`, drawn by the timber code's adzed squared section, D200): a sill plate, a plate and a doubled girt between storeys, a post every `bay` cubes on the grid but none within a metre of a row's end, end posts at inside corners and against other kinds, rails at the kit's heights, under their line in a storey's lower half and over it in its upper so they run into an opening's frame, braces in the lowest and highest panels at each end, a square post up every outside corner, through-wall jambs, heads and sills round every hole, and a gable, the wall carried up into its roof (D241), framed as a triangle by the same rule, not as its cubes' steps (a girt under it, studs over the posts cut off under the roof, rails out to the roof, a brace at each end parallel to the roof); every member 3 cm proud of the daub. **Lime-washed earth** (`limewash.ron`, Poly Haven's `medieval_wall_01`) is earth's rules in a second kind, data only. A framed kind's `core` (`Piece::Wattle`, woven hazel made from a generated image by `tools/textures`, D191) is drawn where a cube is in but not finished, or is being carved, and goes in a layer ahead of the daub; its frame and core go in at a cube's first step (four steps: frame and wattle, then three coats). Framed kinds' floors stand on stone footings, and the footing kind is the block kind of lowest id that is not framed (`Kits::footing`). Meets (D232): earth weighs 3 and runs through timber and wattle, carrying an oak sole plate under them, and stone runs through it; wattle weighs 1 and every other kind runs through it. A further framed kind (plaster, brick nogging) is a kit file. Since D258, the frame's members are the kit's hand-made modules, laid by the same rule. Brick, once a third block kind here, was removed on 2026-10-15 by the developers: "you scope creeped on brick and it looks dumb"; no material is added unless they ask. | Mine, building D227's kits on 2026-10-13: a frame is how wattle and daub, and every half-timbered wall, is built, and laying it by rule from the cubes keeps it one structure with no special pieces, as the role dress does; the frame as data lets plaster and brick nogging follow as files. No library has wattle, so it was generated and tiled as D191 says. Earth first in Poly Haven's `clay_plaster` read as flat orange card (2026-10-13); no Poly Haven material has earth packed in lifts, and ambientCG's scan of layered gritty earth gives the relief and colour that read as hand-built. On brick costing stone, asked on 2026-10-15: "not important right now we're focused on visuals mostly atm"; brick's cost is a placeholder until they take it up. |
| D238 | Laying a base once a change, and making only the detail that is seen | Tech | Agent | Decided | A base is laid whole once, on a worker (`sections::Whole`), and a change lays again only as far as it reaches (D281); each section waiting is cut from it rather than laid alone: what a section holds depends on its whole building (a row of logs' joins, a deck's boards), so laying it alone laid most of the building again for every section. A section is made at the detail it is drawn at and the next coarser it fades into, not every coarser one: within 32 m (36 kept) full and middle detail, to 70 m (76 kept) middle and far, beyond far and proxy, since the coarser is made again before it is seen. Past the middle detail a deck's plank, and at the proxy's distance an undressed log, is a plain box, and far off a band of dress is its profile's three furthest corners: they were most of a far building's triangles. Masks shade a corner's dirt only below its reach, and baking reads sRGB from a table. Measured by `cargo xtask sections` (D185's village, on one thread). | Building step 3's bar (docs/building-plan.md, checking it): a section laid and merged in under 2 ms on a worker; it took 3.5 to 5 ms, about 1 ms of it laying, mostly its building's roof and rows again. Now the median section is cut in 0.01 ms and merged in 1 to 2 ms near, 0.3 ms far, after its base is laid whole in about 2 ms; the village's far details went from 2.9 to 1.1 million triangles. Roofs laid as cubes (D226) will be laid by their neighbourhood and may make the whole-base laying moot; D281 lays a change again only as far as it reaches. |
| D263 | Thick timber walls laid as skins along their faces | Tech | Agent | Decided | A cube of a timber kind whose run across a wall is two or three cubes, each cube of it with its kind either side along the wall, lays its courses along the wall only (`Walls::runs`): a thick wall is skins of logs side by side, one a cube of its thickness, crossing only at its ends and where it turns or another meets it, as a corner does; a pier crosses as a crib. The kit sheet has a row of them, *thick walls and piers*, and a test that no log crosses a thick wall but at its ends. | The goals review (research/goals-review-2026-10-13.md, finding 6) found thick log walls and piers drawing their faces as grids of sawn ends, the stack of boxes D211 rules out; a log wall shows logs along every face, as Enshrouded's rough wood runs its poles along every face whatever the mass behind it. |
| D262 | What makes a generated log read as real | Tech | Agent | Decided | Within D200, a log's face is one material made of two: bark lying in patches over bare wood weathered silver, as a log keeps its bark in places (`textures.ts blend`: Poly Haven's `pine_bark` at half its size, faded, over `rough_wood`, two thirds bark, in soft-edged patches a sixth of the way round and two thirds of a metre along, ragged by the bark's height, the bark proud of the wood with a dark lip), in a texture a metre round by two along, stretched along the log by the kit's `metres`; the bark lost only where a log is exposed (`--exposed`, `timber.exposed`): the texture laid once round each lying log from underneath, its top and its face to the weather (a base's fixed weather way) bare and silvered, a little of its lee face, none underneath, and the log paler toward its ends; its sawn ends real scans (ambientCG's TreeEnd002 to 005, CC0), cut out by their opacity, drawn out round, a little greyed and opened by five radial checks each (`textures.ts ends --checks`), darkened and warmed by the kit's `end_tint`, four in an atlas a log's end picks one of by its seed (a texture's `cells`); a wall's log shaded toward its grooves and lighter on top, and mottled along its length, in its corners' colours (`timber.groove`, `variation.mottle`); a knot or two a log where branches were trimmed, a low swell dark at its heart (`timber.knots`); and the daub mud, in the groove's shade, not a pale strip. Generated logs stay: the fault was the material and the shading, not the shape. Compared with a parametric log module made by a script in Blender, not a modelled log, laid on the same walls as the kit's module (`art/kits/logs`, a `log` module kind in `tools/kits`: round, uneven, lumpy, with branch stubs, D258's `timber.module`), the generated logs read better: the module's copies show a seam each metre, its texture mapped flat from three sides breaks along the log, and its ends' grain is off centre and one picture; the module stays in the kit's pieces, off. This was not the comparison D251 asks for: that is with a whole modelled log, which is still to be made and shown (D200 stays to be compared). Amended by D290 (2026-10-14): a log's face is the modelled Tripo log's bark baked round it (`pine_log`, `tools/kits/bake.py round`), worn point by point to wood weathered silver (`log_wood`, the texture's `worn`, blended through the bark's low places): toward its sawn ends, on its top, and on its face out of the building it walls, the base's fixed weather way only for a wall that walls none. Which side is out is the side not within the building (`layout::enclosed`): something built over the place and something standing each way along the floor, read by column, so a window, a door or a hole knocked through a wall closes it in as the wall round it does and no log turns when one is cut (amended 2026-10-15 by the agent, for the lead: "a window or door shouldn't change which side of a wall is outside"; rooms opened at a hole and turned some 170 logs, 15 to 20 ms on one thread). Rooms stay for light and shelter (D155, D261); `log_bark` retired. | The developers on 2026-10-13: "tbh the log textures still dont look great.. kind of reminds me a bit of our old shitty logs before we added the realism". Beside photographs of real cabins the logs were too dark, one fine brown noise with no medium shapes, striped by bright daub, and capped with one clean picture; real cabin logs are silvered wood with bark left in patches, dark grooves, and greyed, checked ends, which is also how Enshrouded's rough wood reads. |
| D241 | Roofs as cubes, built by hand | Tech | Agent | Decided | As D226 asks, in place of D209's roof sections: a roof is cubes of a roof kind, a kit of `pattern: Roof` numbered from 2048 (`Kind::is_roof`; shakes, slate, thatch, each a file), laid like any cube by hand, with the material picker, brushes and shapes (D231 to D233), or by "add a roof with this material" (`terra_build::roofer`), which lays a plan of them over the space walls close in or a rectangle drawn, a gable (each arm of an L, T or U its own ridge) or a hip (the chessboard distance from the footprint's edge) or a lean-to, slopes, outer and inner corners and, where a span is odd, a ridge's whole cubes, and the walls under its gable ends carried up to it in their own kind. A roof's cubes are no wall tile, floor or room; a room is indoors under them. They hold as any cube does (D193), and a slope also joins the cube a layer below and over whose top its ramp runs on from (`support::ramp`), so a roof climbs a cube a cube with nothing under its slopes. Each goes up in four steps of its fill: frame, battens, covering, trims; a walker stands on its slopes and passes under its underside. Its look is its place among its neighbours (`layout::roofs`): every roof cube with nothing over it is sampled every half cube in four triangles a square, a square carrying on into the next where their corners meet, so roofs at different heights stay apart; creases are ridges, hips and valleys, edges eaves and verges, or flashing where a wall or a higher roof stands beyond; past an open edge the covering is carried on by the kit's `eaves`, drawn only, on the planes of the cube it runs from, meeting the next round a corner as a hip or in a valley; a ridge a cube wide is one cap. D209's stages are kept; since D258 its covering is the cubes' surface in the covering's material and its edges and frame the kit's hand-made modules, not generated courses, caps and boards. A wall under a roof cube is carried up into it, cut at the roof's deck, the plane its covering lies on and its boards under it, eaves and verges are drawn on, so a gable meets its roof in one straight line with no gap: a block kind's surface cut before it is rounded and textured where it ends up, sawn square at the cut, a timber kind's courses with their ends cut to the slope, a log the roof falls across fitting whole under the deck or not at all; a roof cube standing on a wall is that wall to its roles, so no cap, quoin or corner is dressed at the steps a gable's cubes climb in. The roof's frame stops inside the roof (`roofs::framed`): battens and a ridge's beam only over the roof's own cubes and never in a wall or a gable, no rafter along a gable's face or out under a verge, so nothing of it ends in the air past a gable or shows its end through one (2026-10-14). Saves holding D209's roof sections are refused with every older format (D353). Amended by D343: a roof's cube is not put in under a cube that is not a roof's, nor one that is not on a roof's. | Mine, on 2026-10-13, building D226: the developers want to "customize the roof, or add more stories, which means you gotta be able to manually build the roof"; roof cubes on D231's shapes are Enshrouded's roof blocks, any shape and storeys over or beside them, and the convenience D226 keeps ("auto add a roof with [x material]") is a plan of the same cubes. Roof kinds by number keep the 32-bit cube and let support, rooms and walking know a roof without the kits. |
| D251 | One material system for walls, floors and roofs, of hand-made pieces | Design | Developers | Decided | Every kind, wall, floor or roof, is drawn by one system, as Enshrouded's materials are: its cubes' surface in its material's textures (D197), dressed by role (D215) with hand-made pieces modelled in Blender, or from Tripo and cleaned up there, not geometry generated by code. Roof coverings (their courses, ridges, hips, valleys, eaves and verges) and frames (wattle and daub's posts, plates and braces) become kits on that system, in place of the code that generates them now (D236, D241). Amended by D290: geometry the rules shape wearing a hand-made model's bake counts, beside pieces placed whole. | The developers on 2026-10-13, told roof coverings and frames were generated and never compared with hand-made pieces: "i think we want hand made models? enshrouded does this, we are doing what enshrouded does. ideally the same system used for roof materials (and terrain? idk) is whats also the wall material (and floor materials, which might be same as wall materials?)". |
| D252 | Whether the terrain shares building's materials | Design | Agent | Decided | Yes: the terrain and building share one library of materials (textures, height and shading, `assets/materials`, read through D290's reference packs, `assets/data/packs`, and texture arrays), so stone in a wall and stone in a cliff read as the same stone and a new material serves both, while each keeps its own drawing. | D333 | The building baseline: wood and stone, built and destroyed | Design | Developers | Decided | Construction work is held to a minimal baseline until it is flawless and fast: wood (logs, hewn timber, shakes) and stone (stone, slate), built by hand and destroyed, with infinite pretend resources.
