| D246 | The lush forest's floor follows the light | Tech | Agent | Decided | What grows on the lush forest's floor is worked out from the canopy over it, as real floors are: each tree's crown, where its clumps of leaves are (how far it reaches and which way it leans), hides a share of the sky by its kind (an oak's most of it, a Scots pine's much less, a spruce's nearly all, a young tree's less), thinned in gaps by noise, with light down the paths' corridors. Grass grows by that light: thick, with tall patches, in clearings, gaps and along paths; under a closed canopy only in tufts where a fleck falls, thin grass in clumps rather than a stubble, the floor there fallen leaves under broadleaves, needles under conifers (Poly Haven's Forest Ground 03, a sixth layer of the ground) and moss in damp hollows; thinner and drier on needles. The cover map gains a second picture, of needles, duff and how much canopy stands over, which dims the ground's sky light in place of the leaf litter (D216). An eye-height layer of ten kinds, six variants each in tints of their own and scaled at random: bracken (fronds rising and arching out), ferns (the scan and grown fronds), herbs, hazel, bilberry under the pines, oak scrub, saplings, dead branches and flowers; placed as a Thomas process, drift centres where the light, the trunks, the logs and each kind's own noise like it and a Gaussian clump round each. Fallen logs taper, lumpy and burred, broken off at the crown's end in splinters, sawn with weathered end grain or broken at the foot, with stubs of branches, dark and wet below, of pine bark among the conifers; stumps are generated with spreading roots, sawn or broken; rocks are clustered round boulders, sunk a fifth to two fifths of their height once their bounds are known, and drawn with moss over what faces up (`mossy.wesl`). On the RTX 3080 the walk bench is 11.9 ms median and 13.4 ms at the 99th percentile, within budget, with the canopy of D247 (another agent's bench on the same GPU doubles it, so measure alone); bare ground at the vantage views 6.8% at the floor view, all of it path, and at the forest's edge 37% to none; lodcheck (worst 0.07% a frame) and collide pass. |
| D247 | The lush forest's trees: a closed canopy, leaves lit through, bark in proportion | Tech | Agent | Decided | Amends D235. **Canopy:** grown oaks stouter (trunks 3.8 to 4.6% of their height through) with crowns as wide as they are tall or wider, starting lower; each leans toward and reaches farther to a side it found light on, its great limbs unevenly up the trunk, the lowest one or two the greatest, near level and long; leaves that sag below 70% of the bole are shed. | D264 | Trees under and close: leaves lit through every layer, bark at its real albedo, roots and modelled trunks in the grown bark | Tech | Agent | Decided | Amends D221, D235, D247. **Leaves:** a crown's cards all have normals leaning out of it, so from inside or below one shows its far side; there it reflects 0.55 as much, takes the sky's light as the leaves round it bounce it (green, not blue) and no specular, since Bevy's Fresnel at a normal turned from the eye reflected the whole sky off it; a crown's lower face gets 0.4 of the painted sky's light from below, which assumes sunlit ground, not a shaded forest floor. | D270 | Sky light under the canopy from a volume baked from the crowns | Tech | Agent | Decided | Everything under and among a forest's crowns takes its diffuse sky light from a Bevy irradiance volume (`terra_world::sky_occlusion`) instead of the open sky's environment map: the layer above gives its crowns as ellipsoids, each 4 by 2.5 m cell is given how much of the sky each of its six sides sees through them, once, on another thread, and each time the sky is painted again the volume holds the open sky's light for what is seen, the leaves' green glow for what is hidden, and the ground's bounce, dimmed in deep shade. Past the forest and above it the volume sees the open sky; Bevy drops the environment map's multiple-scattering term where a volume lights a surface, so ground there is some 15% dimmer and leaves as much brighter than the map gives, a seam left in the meadow 70 m past the edge. The camera's boom meets trunks and rocks since D274; shrubs are not solid (D243). | A review of the lush forest's film (5.5/10), its first gap: 8 of 20 frames with a shrub or spruce over 30% of the screen at the lens, one all green, the player running through a hazel as if it were not there, fronds sweeping the lens. | D272 | Leaves cut by their picture alone, and lit through by the sky above them | Tech | Agent | Decided | A leaf's alpha in the main pass is its picture's: Bevy takes a vertex's colour, alpha too, into the base colour that the alpha test cuts by, and the crowns' vertex alpha is how much sky a point sees (D216), while the prepass ignores vertex colours; so where a leaf saw little sky the main pass cut it and the prepass kept its depth, and it stood black against the sky in its own shape. The foliage shader divides that alpha out again. And a leaf passes on, in its own colour, the light that falls on its upper face, as the sky occlusion volume (D270) holds it there: Bevy's own diffuse transmission took the open sky, unshaded by the crowns, and the colour after the underside was darkened. | Review of 2026-10-14: crowns from below read as "black paper cutouts"; near-black was a fifth of the view looking up, from 3.6% before the steeper crown insides of D269 sent more of their leaves below the cut. | D275 | The forest floor in layers that settle into each other, with litter lying on it | Tech | Agent | Decided | Amends D246 and D268. **The ground's layers** (`assets/shaders/ground.wesl`) are scans of what a wood's floor is: Poly Haven's Brown Mud 02 for trodden soil and paths, Dry Decay Leaves for leaf litter, Forest Leaves 04 for needle duff, Roots, and ambientCG's Moss 004 for moss, eight layers with each one's height, its broad swells taken out, in its normal map's blue (`textures.ts stack --height`). The floor's layers are sampled hex-tiled, as Mikkelsen's "Practical Real-Time Hex-Tiling" (JCGT 2022, MIT, ported), so no repeat shows, and laid on each other by height, as terrain splatting in shipped games does, so leaves cover soil leaf by leaf and moss fills the hollows; the cover map is read a little way off by noise, so its edges fray rather than ending in half-metre blobs; hollows are darker and see less of the sky, the light under a canopy (D270), and the relief is half as strong again. **What covers it**: fallen leaves wherever trees stand near, off the paths, not only in deep shade; needles carpet a conifer stand between its trees, giving way under a broadleaf's crown; grass in the shade thins into the litter rather than ending. **Litter as geometry** (`terra_world::cover::litter`), as shipped games scatter debris over their terrain: cards of an atlas composed from ambientCG's scanned leaves (`textures.ts cards`, `litter` recipes in `art/textures/litter`: oak and beech leaves, needles, cones, twigs, ivy) lying humped on the ground in the grass's nearest ring, some 3.5 a square metre on a full floor and twice that against trunks, logs and rocks, ivy in drifts under broadleaves, none in thick grass, sunk into the ground a piece at a time from 8 to 16 m (`litter.wesl`), drawn with no sheen as the canopy hides the sky it would reflect. Dead branches lie as limbs sunk into the litter, forked, some forks propping a thin end up, in brown bark; a trunk's foot has moss in cushions on what faces up and soil over its bark between (`foliage_fragment.wesl` `weather`). How much grass grows under the canopy and the litter's colour are D293; heaps against trunks, rocks' moss and the floor in the sun D294. | A review against Sons of the Forest (the ground 4.5/10): flat orange-tan ground in the pine stand with no needles, a near-black featureless floor under the oaks, grey sticks, a root flare a smooth green cone, the path one repeating pebble texture, the dirt a hard-edged blob. Measured on the vantage sheet's floor views, the lowest 30% of the picture: small-scale contrast (each 9-pixel square's SD over its mean) 0.21 to 0.29 on the path at 13:00 and 0.20 to 0.24 on the floor at 17:00, featureless pixels 3.1% to 0.6%, the path's strongest repeat 0.20 to 0.10; a harsh review scored the ground 4 before, 5.5 after (still a printed rug beside Sons of the Forest's heaps), its litter texture swapped for larger leaves after it; bare ground within 30 m 7.0% to 6.1%, all of it path. | D276 | The lush forest's leaves at their own size, its pines each their own, spruce cards drawn needle by needle | Tech | Agent | Decided | Amends D247, D268 and D269. **Leaves:** a composed card says how long its leaves are as a share of it (`leaf` in `foliage.ron`, measured by `textures.ts cards`), and a plant is grown so that a leaf is its real size near the camera: hazel, oak and the oak branch tip are composed with more, smaller leaves (14%, 14% and 9% of a card, from 20, 19 and 18%), so a hazel's leaf is 8 to 9 cm, a sapling's 10, a young oak's 10 and an old oak's, high in the canopy, 14; the floor's hazel, once a dome of clumps grown to 1.1 to 2.1 times its size, is young stools at their own. **Shrubs:** hazel grows as stools of four forms, so no two read as one dome: a vase leaning out all round, some stems bowed over; a fan leaning to the light; rods a few years from the cut on bare poles; and overstood stems like small trees from one root; each holds its leaves in flat sprays along its shoots, not balls of cards, a quarter of them missing, so the stems show between. Oak scrub is two to four small spreading oaks from one root. **Spruce:** its card is drawn needle by needle (`needles` recipes in `leaves.ts`): a twig and side shoots clothed all round in needles 2 cm long on a young spruce, those behind its twig darker and the young tips lighter, so its edge is a fringe of single needles rather than a picture's cut outline of tooth-like shoots. **Pines:** eight kinds, pointed, flat-topped, forked and broken (`Form`), each with its own girth (1.6 to 2.6% of its height in radius), lean (a sixtieth to a seventh of its height at the top, one in four hard), bow, crown depth, reach, outline and lopsidedness, the height its grey-brown bark turns to orange plates and how orange, and three to seven grey dead branches under its live crown; each placed 0.85 to 1.18 times as stout as the next of its kind; grown pines stand in knots of a few, 2.7 m apart at the least, with open ground between, on a finer field, while spruces keep their room, since their skirts would close a knot into a wall. Kinds added later are added after the rest, so each keeps the seed it was grown from. **Walks:** a scripted route walk stopped by a trunk or a rock heads off to one side and on again, half as long again each try (to eight seconds), three tries to a side before the other, and gives a spot up after twenty seconds for the next, so a layout that changes does not leave the bench walking into a tree, nor a film stood at a boulder for thirty seconds (review of 2026-10-14). | The lush forest's fourth adversarial review, 2026-10-14: "a dozen identical straight salmon-orange trunks of the same diameter and spacing ... reads as copy-paste", "spruces ... made of cards with saw-tooth cut edges", hazel and shrub leaves "about 3 to 4 times the size of real leaves", hazel "a rounded topiary dome". Measured before: hazel leaves 13 cm on a stool and 12 to 38 cm on the floor's bushes, a young oak's 21 cm and an old oak's 28; pines' trunks 0.53 to 0.6 m in radius over four kinds, every one within 3% of its height of upright, nearest neighbours 4.7 m at the least, cv 0.34. After: girth's spread over its height cv 0.16 from 0.06, leans 2 to 13% of the height, nearest neighbours cv 0.48, 2.7 m at the least. Walk bench 14.6 ms median, 17.5 ms 99th percentile on the RTX 3080, main 16.3 ms in the same contended hour; lodcheck passes (worst 0.13%). |
| D285 | A third-person camera that keeps calm among trunks | Tech | Agent | Decided | Amends D274 and D267. As shipped cameras keep collision apart from occlusion (Cinemachine's Deoccluder, Unreal's spring arm on a camera channel trees ignore; research/foliage-near-camera.md): **the boom** (`terra_game` `play/camera.rs`) is swept only against build walls, the ground and what is broad, 2.5 m across or more and not a tree (`Solid::breadth`): a boulder; trees and small things crossing the line to the figure do not pull it in, they hide the figure for a moment. **The lens** is a 0.3 m sphere that never goes into any solid: it moves from where it was to the boom's end by collide-and-slide, round a trunk in its way, comes back to the end at a rate of 2 a second once past, and slid aside, turns to keep the figure where it was in the picture. A trunk that hides the figure's chest and head from the boom's end for 0.6 s has the boom ease in front of it (a rate of 2.5 a second, no shorter than 2 m) until the figure has been in sight from the end for 0.5 s. The shoulder swap is gone; the boom stays over the right shoulder. **A tree's own solid**, what the lens keeps out of, is its bole, its wood taller than a body; its root flare is added as extra pieces, as a skirt is (D248), so it still stops a walker as before. | A review of the forest's films (6.0/10), its first gap: the boom pumped, the player's skull filling the frame for 0.2 s and springing back, about eight times in twenty seconds among veteran oaks, the player once out of the frame. | D334 | The air's scale is the world's | Tech | Agent | Decided | Amends D212 and D299. Every distance through the air is `AirScale::for_world` times the world's: as far through the air as the small planet's farthest ground was (its horizon from its highest hill times 18, about 14 km), for the farthest ground a world shows (the horizon from its highest ground), never less than the true air and never more than 18. The small planet, the lush forest's rolling ground and the flat 30 km yard keep 18; the 30 km world with mountains 2.5 km high sees 12 km and has nearly the true air. Under trees the forest's scale stays half the open air's (D299), never under 1; out in the open the haze lets one see as far through the air as before, so farther in the world, and a world whose views reach kilometres with no canopy volume stands in the open air rather than the forest's haze. The dust (Mie) is held per world metre instead, 1.5 times a clear day's at 18 and so about 25 times at the mountains' 1.08: a hazy summer's day with some 40 km of visibility, its far ridges whitened and its sky pale, while the gases' blue is the true air's. | At 18 times, the mountain lake's camera 1.3 km over the sea stood 24 km up the air, its sky near black, and a peak 5 km off hazed as 90 km of real air (docs/demos.md, the air's scale); the forest's 240 m haze, which a world with no canopy volume kept, hid everything past the shore. | D269 | Leaf cards composed from scanned leaves, with a normal map | Tech | Agent | Decided | Amends D222. A broadleaf card is composed, as studios build their SpeedTree and Unreal foliage atlases, from single leaves cut out of ambientCG's CC0 leaf scans (`textures.ts cards` with a `<card>.leaves.json` recipe, `tools/textures/leaves.ts`): a twig and its shoots, or a cane with leaf stalks of three or five leaflets, the leaves laid along them alternately at angles, sizes and turns of their own, foreshortened as they turn and tilt, some young and yellower toward the tips, some old and darker olive toward the base, a few turned to show a paler underside, drawn back to front with a soft shadow where one lies on another, darker toward their stalks. Beside the colour atlas the tool writes its normal map (`normal.png`, OpenGL's convention), each leaf's scanned relief turned and tilted as it is laid, flat for cards drawn from pictures. Hazel (LeafSet024), oak and the oak branch tip (LeafSet016), bilberry with its blue-black berries (LeafSet001) and bramble (LeafSet014) are made so; holly keeps its picture, its yellowed leaves toned to olive; cards touched by hand since (the spruce, D248) are kept as the atlas has them (`keep`). Oak's spring scan is shifted toward summer's deeper green (`hue`). **Shading:** the foliage shader leans the crown's bent normal by each leaf's own, from the atlas's normal map laid on the card's face (Schüler's cotangent frame from screen derivatives, since a card's tangents carry its drop-out, D223), 0.8 of the way (`relief`), so a crown still shades as one mass while its leaves catch the sun one by one, and glow through by their own tilt. A crown's or shrub's inside sees little of the sky: `Canopy::sky` falls off steeply inside its outermost leaves, measured by the crown's own envelope direction by direction (an egg of holly or a vase of hazel is deep in by its shape, not as a ball), the lush oaks' likewise (conifers as they were); the shader's darkening of the inside leaves the skin alone; a leaf in another crown's shadow passes on 0.06 of the sun (from 0.2, D264), so shadowed leaves read dark against those a fleck lights. The middle layer is shaded as a crown, its inside dark and its leaves lit through (0.8). Card to card a leaf is lighter or darker by a fifth, an eighth young and yellower, more than a quarter old and darker olive. **Shrubs:** the middle layer draws every card of every clump near, and the trees' middle level past 28 m. | D212 | How the air, the exposure and plants' materials are drawn | Tech | Agent | Decided | The sky's colour, the sun's colour through the air and the haze over distance come from Bevy's physical atmosphere (Hillaire 2020, as Unreal's sky atmosphere), with Earth's air as Hillaire gives it and a little more dust, centred below the camera and seen with every distance through it 18 times the world's, since the stand-in planet is 2 km round (`terra_world::air`). Our sky dome draws the clouds, lit by the sun through the air, the stars and the moon over it, and the dusk's and night's own colours once the sun is down, as the atmosphere goes dark at sunset. The camera is exposed for sunlit ground, EV 10.8 with a low sun and 11.8 with a high one, opening to 9 at night through the dusk, keyed by the sun's height with the rest of the light (`daylight`); AgX tone mapping; screen-space ambient occlusion over 1.2 m, with D204's contact shadows; a faint bloom (0.03, since an energy-conserving bloom blends that much of a wide blur into every pixel and at 0.1 washed the middle distance flat). The sun's light is given as it is above the air and the atmosphere dims and reddens it. Trees, bushes and rocks are drawn in real materials, a texture stack of bark, leaves and lichen-covered rock (`materials/flora`) named per part in `flora.ron`, projected triplanar as the ground is, tinted by the kind's colour, with the free models' flat-shaded normals smoothed across curves and split at creases. | The developers: "the graphics/lighting still dont look like our art goals fwiw (closer to realism)" and "you can just fix the core textures if you need to (and the lighting) to make our test scene match up" (2026-10-13). The scene was over-exposed and washed out, ACES turned greens saturated and flat, the sky was a painted wash with distance fog of one colour, and plants were flat colours. A physical atmosphere is how shipped engines draw sky and aerial perspective, and Bevy ships one; a fixed exposure keyed by the time of day is how games with a day cycle hold a look, cheaper and steadier than eye adaptation; AgX is the film-like response Blender moved to from Filmic. Triplanar materials texture models with no UVs without remodelling them. |
